**Book 250:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas I* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-875-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 1 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (Fase A outcomes and ATP, printed pp. 1–14) plus one guide section per student chapter, with objectives, lesson flow and timing, activities, misconception notes, remedial and enrichment tasks, project rubrics, answer keys keyed to named student-book pages, and reproducible ten-frame, number-bond and game templates (printed pp. 1–200). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. The guide reproduces the student book's chapter covers, placeholder ISBN and all; its own ISBN is as given here. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 251:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 252:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Iqbal Helmi, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-909-6. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 253:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 254:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas VI* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dicky Susanto, Savitri Sihombing, Ambarsari Kusuma Wardani, Ummy Salmah, Eunice Salim, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-900-4. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Classifying triangles by their sides (scalene, isosceles, equilateral) and by their largest angle (acute-angled, obtuse-angled, right-angled); the angles of a triangle sum to 180° (each angle of an equilateral triangle is 60°); naming a quadrilateral by its vertices in order; the parallelogram's properties — opposite sides parallel and equal, opposite angles equal, the diagonals bisect each other; rectangle, rhombus and square characterised inside the parallelogram family by their diagonals — diagonals that bisect each other give a parallelogram, bisecting and equal give a rectangle, bisecting and perpendicular give a rhombus, bisecting, equal and perpendicular give a square; the trapezium — a quadrilateral with one pair of parallel sides (the two bases) — with the isosceles trapezium's equal legs and equal base angles, and the segment joining the midpoints of the legs parallel to the bases and equal to half the sum of their lengths | Syria, Grade 6 pupil's book, printed pp. 28–31, 49–56, 69–73, 79–82 |

| Joining the midpoints of any quadrilateral's sides in order always gives a parallelogram (Varignon's figure, met on an exercise page); the pyramid defined — a polygon base, an apex S outside the base's plane, triangular lateral faces and the lateral surface; the height is the perpendicular segment from the apex to the base plane (its foot may fall inside or outside the base) and is a different segment from the slant height of a lateral face; a regular pyramid — regular-polygon base, apex above the centre, congruent isosceles lateral faces; the regular tetrahedron — all four faces equilateral triangles; the cone of revolution (المخروط الدوراني) from rotating a right triangle about one of its legs — apex, height, generator and disc base; nets of a pyramid and of a cone — the cone's lateral surface unrolls to a circular sector whose arc length equals the base circle's circumference | Syria, Grade 8 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 48, 95–105 |

| Triangles and quadrilaterals by sides/vertices — shapes (Дүрс): join geoboard dots into a broken line; a girl built shapes from 3 sticks (1, 3), a boy from 4 sticks (2, 4, 5); a shape with 3 sides and 3 vertices is a triangle (гурвалжин), with 4 sides and 4 vertices a quadrilateral (дөрвөн өнцөгт) — vertex and side labelled; identify nine pictured shapes | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 26 |
| Polygons: pentagon and hexagon — classify the earlier shapes in tables (four-sided / three-sided; four vertices / three vertices) and answer how many of each; can a shape have 3 sides and 4 vertices? — find the error; build a triangle and a quadrilateral on a geoboard; draw three triangles and three quadrilaterals with ruler and pencil; count sides and vertices of ten polygons into a table; a 5-vertex 5-side shape is a pentagon (таван өнцөгт), a 6-vertex 6-side shape a hexagon (зургаан өнцөгт) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 27 |
| Naming polygons in a table — copy and complete the table: triangle, quadrilateral (given), pentagon (… өнцөгт), hexagon; complete each pictured angle into a quadrilateral; count which shapes appear how many times in a house picture (door = quadrilateral noted); build animals with the tangram set — who can build the most animals? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 28 |
| Rectangle and square (Тэгш өнцөгт, Квадрат): fold paper as shown to make a quadrilateral; measure the angles at every vertex of the made quadrilateral — a quadrilateral all of whose angles are right angles is a rectangle (Дөрвөн өнцөг нь дөрвүүлээ тэгш байна); fold a rectangular paper and compare the side lengths, fold it into a quadrilateral and measure its angles; fold a made right-angled shape and compare side lengths — a quadrilateral whose four angles are all right and whose four sides are all equal is a square | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 29 |
| Polygons: sides, vertices, angles — a picture of a furnished room: what shapes do we know about? Find the shapes from the picture above and name them; a. write in your notebook each shape's number, its sides, vertices and number of angles (18 numbered shapes: a square, a hexagon, a triangle, a pentagon, an arrow-like pentagon, a rectangle, a trapezoid, a wide trapezoid, a rectangle, a parallelogram, a hexagon, an arrow shape, a right triangle, a long triangle, a triangle, a hexagon, a rhombus-like pentagon, a notched pentagon): what can be said about one shape's numbers of sides, vertices and angles (they are equal)?; b. draw the table and sort the shapes by their numbers into it (columns: triangular (3-angled); quadrilateral; pentagonal; hexagonal; heptagonal); c. name the shapes; a boy's speech: "These are flat, closed shapes. Each side of a shape is a straight line segment. Most shapes can be named by their number of angles"; g. separate the shapes by the given properties and write the numbers in the table (rows: has a right angle / has no right angle; columns: has mutually perpendicular sides / has parallel sides; the square, number 1, is entered); d. which shape has how many right angles?; e. how do shapes 3, 13 and 15 differ from each other? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 35 |
| Open shapes, polygons by name, symbol algebra — name the shapes (an open square outline, an open triangle outline, an arrow outline, an open hexagon outline): how do they differ from the previous shapes? Are these closed shapes (битуу дүрс)?; measure the lengths of the segments (three coloured segments) and write the measurements in your notebook; number the shapes drawn on squared paper (7 shapes: a rhombus, a rectangle, a stepped shape, a trapezoid, a wide rectangle, a T-shape, a slanted pentagon): a. classify the shapes by the given property, sort them and write the numbers in the table (rows: four-sided / not four-sided; columns: all angles are right angles / not all angles are right angles; number 2 is entered); b. how many sides and angles do shapes 3 and 6 have? Name the shapes; a girl's speech: "Shapes with four angles, five angles, six angles, eight angles and other such shapes are called by another name — polygon (олон өнцөгт)"; in each picture, find the shapes you have seen, and write their names and numbers (composite pictures: a big triangle divided by a line; a trapezoid divided into a triangle and a quadrilateral; a hexagon divided by diagonals into triangles); name the shapes: what number does each shape represent (a symbol grid: trapezoid + circle + trapezoid = 26; pentagon + circle + circle = 33; triangle + circle + arrow = 23; column sums: trapezoid + pentagon + triangle = 31, circle + circle + circle = 24, so the circle = 8, and the other symbols' values are to be found; then do the operations: trapezoid + circle + arrow + pentagon = ?; 8 × trapezoid + 9 × arrow − triangle = ?; 50 ÷ triangle + 56 ÷ circle − pentagon = ?); compose a similar problem yourself | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 36 |
| The cube: faces, edges, vertices — the cube, recognising its net (Куб, түүний дэлгээсийг таних): pictured are a tissue box, a pink box, a gift box, a white box, a wooden crate and a plain box: what does the picture show? What shape are these?; study the box: a. prepare or find a box of the shape above; b. pay attention to what are called its faces (талс), vertices (орой) and edges (ирмэг) — a labelled cube diagram; c. place each of the box's faces on paper in turn, draw around its boundary: how many shapes did you draw in all? What are the shapes' sizes like?; count the box's edges (pictures of a die with its faces numbered 1-6 and of a cube's wire frame): how many edges are there? How many edges are of equal length?; d. how many vertices does the box have?; e. write what you found in the table (columns: the solid's name; the number of faces; the number of edges; the number of vertices; the faces' shape: square); a box at the right states: "Cube (шоо). A cube is a solid (биет)"; write the numbers of vertices and faces of the drawn shapes in the table (a flat yellow square and a cube drawn side by side: shape name; number of faces; number of vertices); in the pictured drawings, is the number of a solid's vertices equal to something? Discuss (the square and the cube compared); find square-shaped and cube-shaped things around you and write them down; a make-and-do task: a. from paper make a square; from plasticine make a cube; b. from plasticine, straws (соруул) and sticks (савх), make a square and a cube (frame models are pictured) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 77 |
| Trapezoids, parallelograms, rhombuses: folding and classifying quadrilaterals — find the trapezoid- and parallelogram-shaped shapes around you and name them; an activity: fold the paper following the given sequence in the picture, cut it and unfold it (the rectangle folded in four, a corner folded and cut away, opening into a diamond: a. measure the sides and angles of the shape formed; b. discuss the property of the shape formed: this shape is called a rhombus (ромбо)); the three named shapes with their lettered vertices — the trapezoid (Трапец), the rhombus (Ромбо) and the parallelogram (Параллелограмм): a. name each shape's parallel sides; b. measure the sides' lengths and write the conclusion; draw quadrilaterals by joining the points on the geo-paper (the dotted geoboard with a trapezoid and a parallelogram begun); name the 4 quadrilaterals formed and discuss their properties (the strip of five shapes laid over the coloured rectangles: the rectangle, the square, the triangle, the parallelogram and the rhombus); draw the Altan-khad (Алхан хээ) pattern in your notebook (the traditional meander pattern drawn on the squared grid); the morin khuur's body (цар) is like which shape (a trapezoid — with the photograph of the morin khuur monument in Dundgovi aimag, whose height equals a four-storey building) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 88 |
| Classifying triangles and polygons (Гурвалжин ба олон өнцөгтийг ангилах): an activity: let's learn to classify a triangle by its angles: measure the angles of the triangles formed on the geoboard and write the measures in your notebook: classify the triangles by grouping the like ones, and mark the result in the table (the eight geoboard triangles, and the table's three columns: one angle is right; one angle is obtuse; all the angles are acute); a boy's summary: "If one angle is right, it is a right-angled triangle; if one angle is obtuse, it is an obtuse-angled triangle; if all the angles are acute, it is called an acute-angled triangle"; observe how the shapes are classified and discuss it (the two boxes: Polygon (Олон өнцөгт) — a shape with three or more sides and angles is called a polygon (the triangle, quadrilateral and pentagon pictured); Not a polygon (Олон өнцөгт биш) — if one of its sides is a curved line, it is not called a polygon (the circle, the round-topped shape and the shape with a curved side pictured)); measure the triangle's sides and discuss (a. the triangle with all its sides marked equal: a regular triangle (Зөв гурвалжин); b. the triangle with unequal sides: an irregular triangle (Зөв биш гурвалжин); a boy's note: "A triangle whose sides, or whose angles, are all equal is called a regular triangle"); measure the polygons' sides and discuss (a. name the irregular polygons (the quadrilateral (Дөрвөн өнцөгт), the pentagon (Таван өнцөгт), the hexagon (Зургаан өнцөгт), and the heptagon and octagon whose names are to be supplied); b. name the regular polygons (the square — a regular quadrilateral, that is a square (Зөв дөрвөн өнцөгт буюу квадрат); the regular pentagon (Зөв таван өнцөгт); and the regular hexagon, heptagon and octagon); a boy's note: "A regular polygon's sides are all equal and its angles are all equal"); name the following regular polygons and write the regular polygon's side count (the pictured octagon, equilateral triangle, quadrilateral, pentagon and hexagon); if a regular hexagon's side length is 8 cm, find its frame length (48 cm); if a regular triangle's frame length is 42 cm, find its side's length (14 cm) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 89–90 |

| Decomposing figures — decomposing a big triangle and a trapezoid into other figures; drawing figures full of other figures; which figures appear in a figure of the number 100 | Venezuela, Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más... (Grade 2), printed pp. 26–27 |
| Shapes in the world — finding triangles, rectangles and other plane figures in the schoolyard and classroom (flags, hoops, diagonals of door frames) | Venezuela, Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más... (Grade 2), printed pp. 28–29 |

| Properties of parallelograms — essential properties: opposite sides equal, opposite angles equal; rectángulo (right angles, opposite sides equal), rombo (four equal sides), cuadrado (equal sides and right angles) defined; square construction as a regular 4-gon | Venezuela, Contando con los recursos (Grade 4), printed pp. 126–129 |
| Diagonals of parallelograms — diagonal investigations posed as questions (equality, bisection, perpendicularity in the rhombus); a word problem (Luis <4 sides = triangle; María double = hexagon; Cheo +2 = pentagon; Valentina = María −2 and regular = square, the parallelogram) | Venezuela, Contando con los recursos (Grade 4), printed pp. 130–133 |

| Classification — Parallelogram family: rectangle (four right angles), square, rhombus (four equal sides); a square is both a rhombus and a rectangle | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 122–123 |
| Parallelograms — Diagonals of parallelograms bisect each other (measurement discovery on the geoboard) | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed p. 123 |
| Polygons — Polygons classified by number of interior angles (triángulo … decágono); a regular polygon has equal sides and equal angles | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed pp. 107 |

| Prisms — The prism defined: two congruent polygon bases in parallel planes joined by parallelogram faces; the honeycomb cell as a hexagonal prism | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed p. 151 |
| Regular polygons — Regular polygons: all sides congruent AND all angles congruent — both conditions are required | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed p. 152 |

| Kites — The kite (cometa): a quadrilateral whose consecutive sides are congruent in pairs, built from two diagonals crossing at right angles | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed pp. 200–201 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 70 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Tanzania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Peru, Serbia).**.** Jordan carries the full sorting ladder Iran names shapes by stencil in Grade 1, works with flat and solid shapes in Grade 2, classifies quadrilaterals in Grade 4 and polygons in Grade 8, and gives constructions and polygons again in Grade 10 Geometry (1).
— polygons, triangles two ways, then the parallelogram family
with its special cases. Egypt carries first plane shapes and
shape-making in Primary 1 (Japanese-collaboration series),
2-dimensional shape attributes in Primary 2 (old Discover),
polygons by vertices in
Grade 3 and the triangle/trapezium/kite sorting in Grade 5
(rows completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep).
Greece carries polygons including regular polygons in Grade 6.
Armenia carries the proof-level triangle and quadrilateral
chapters in Geometry 7 and 8. Kazakhstan carries polygons and
rectangle/square in Grade 2. Vietnam carries the shape names in
Grade 3, parallelogram and rhombus in Grade 4, and the
quadrilaterals chapter in Grade 8. Sierra Leone carries the
plane-shape review with equal-side marks and vertex-letter
naming in JSS 1. Mauritania carries rectangle, triangle kinds,
parallelogram and rhombus in Grade 4. Libya carries 2D shapes —
quadrilaterals, polygons, squares and rectangles — in Grade 4.
South Africa carries triangles, polygons and angles in Grade 9.
Ghana's evidence is teacher-manual only (JICA B5 Lessons 89–91):
no pupil textbook in Ghana's set teaches shapes, and the lesson
words Ghana that way throughout. Laos carries shapes from everyday solids and stick-and-tile shapes in Grade 1, through triangles, quadrilaterals and nets in Grades 2–4, to polygons, quadrilateral properties and solid nets in Grade 5 and the full quadrilateral and triangle range in Grades 6–7. Yemen carries shapes from naming solids and plane shapes in Grade 1, through polygons, rectangles, squares and triangles, to parallelograms, rhombuses, trapeziums and the full set of solids in Grades 6–7. Indonesia carries shapes from three shapes only in Grade 1 (triangle, quadrilateral, curved shape — no circle as a shape), through polygons and the five solids in Grade 2, triangles and quadrilaterals including the trapezium in Grade 4, the full quadrilateral comparison with the inclusive trapezium definition in Grade 5, cubes and cuboids treated spatially in Grade 6, to solid classification and nets in Grade 9. Note the two trapezium definitions in Indonesia's own books: Grade 4 defines the trapezium with exactly one pair of parallel sides (p. 162), while Grade 5 uses the inclusive definition under which a parallelogram counts as a trapezium. Nicaragua carries shapes whole from Grade 1 — solids recognised by their flat and curved parts in the student book, plane figures in the old guide's complementary unit (guide evidence), solids and plane figures in Grade 2, circles and triangles in Grade 3, triangles, quadrilaterals and prisms in Grade 4, polygons and prisms/cylinders in Grade 5, regular polygons and pyramids/cones in Grade 6, the rebuilt geometry of Grade 7 and parallelograms in Grade 8, to the regular polyhedra and solids of revolution in Grade 10. Ethiopia carries shapes from naming solids and counting sides and vertices in Grade 1, through flat shapes by side count and dividing and combining them in Grade 2, moving shapes and symmetry and polygons by side count in Grade 3, lines, angles, triangles and quadrilaterals in Grade 4, lines, angles and the common solids in Grade 5, triangles, quadrilaterals, the circle and solids with nets in Grade 6, the quadrilaterals revised in Grade 7, to regular polygons with their angle sums and areas in Grade 9 — with the Grade 2 book's "rectangular pyramid" corrected to rectangular prism throughout. South Sudan carries shapes from drawing, colouring and counting shapes in Grade 1, through shape properties in Grades 2–3, to 3D-shape properties and nets in Grade 6, quadrilaterals and triangle types in Grade 7, pyramids and prisms in Grade 8, and triangles, polygons, quadrilaterals and solids in Secondary 1 — with the honest limits that Primary 1 teaches no 3-D shapes despite its content map promising them, and the Primary 3 book's rectangle-activity slips corrected (join the points to make the rectangle complete; opposite sides equal). Iraq carries shapes from solids and plane shapes in Grade 1, through sides, vertices, faces and forming and drawing shapes in Grades 2–3, to parallel and perpendicular lines, triangle classification and constructions in Grade 5, parallelograms, trapeziums, composite shapes and tessellation in Grade 6, and regular polygons, solids and composite solids in Grades 7–9. Bangladesh carries shapes from round, triangular and quadrangular shapes in everyday objects in Grade 1, through quadrilaterals and the square-is-a-rectangle rule in Grade 3, triangles by sides and by angles in Grade 4, the parallelogram family and the circle in Grade 5, 3-D models and boxes in Grade 6, triangle construction and properties in Grade 7, similar triangles and quadrilateral construction in Grade 8, to cones, spheres, prisms and pyramids in Grade 9. Nepal carries shapes from naming the circle, triangle and quadrilateral in Grade 1, through triangles, quadrilaterals and circles made with sticks and straws in Grade 2 and line segments, angles, triangles and quadrilaterals in Grade 3, to solids with faces, edges and vertices in Grade 4, plane figures, circles and solids in Grade 6, triangles, quadrilaterals, polygons and solids in Grades 7–8, and triangle, quadrilateral and construction work in Grades 9–10. Cambodia carries shapes whole from recognising triangles, rectangles, squares and circles in Grade 1 through quadrilaterals, polygons and triangle centres in Grades 7–9, with triangle and quadrilateral area formulas and solid figures in Grade 10. Kuwait carries shapes from solids and plane shapes in Grades 1–4, through basic geometry in Grade 5 and triangles, quadrilaterals and polygons in Grade 6, to constructions in Grade 7 and a full quadrilaterals unit in Grade 8. North Macedonia carries shapes from the very first geometry at Grade 1 (2022) — lines, the square, triangle and rectangle, and the ball, cube, cuboid, cylinder and cone with their faces — to space geometry in Grade 8 (2010 edition): points, lines and planes in space, skew lines, and the prism.

China carries shapes from Grade 1 plane shapes and the tangram, through Grade 9 projections and three views, to the senior solid-geometry chapters in both editions.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries shapes at every level — the Primer's and Grade 1's first shapes, the primary solids and their face/edge/vertex counts, Classes 6–7's classifications, Class 9's proved parallelogram theorems and the Class 12 conics.

Syria names solids and their faces in Grades 1–2, counts faces, vertices and edges in Grade 3, and then studies quadrilaterals as one family — the parallelogram in Grade 5, its properties used and proved in Grades 6–7, rectangle, rhombus and square recognised by their diagonals — while triangles are classified by sides and by angles. Grade 8 adds the pyramid, the regular tetrahedron and the cone of revolution with their nets, and Grade 9 the regular polygon with its central angle 360°/n.

Saudi Arabia names shapes from Grade 1 — solids sorted by roll/stack/slide, plane shapes by sides and vertices — counts faces, edges and vertices in Grade 2, classifies solids and names polygons to the nonagon in Grade 3, and in Grade 4 adds points, lines, perpendiculars and parallels and classifies triangles two ways, with the older 1445 Term-3 volumes witnessing the Part Two chapters.

Malaysia names and classifies shapes in Form 1 (polygons, triangles, quadrilaterals) and returns in Form 2 for regular polygons and for the three-dimensional shapes and their nets.

Rwanda teaches shapes across the primary years and into Senior 1: Year 2 names squares, rectangles and triangles, Year 3 gives their properties and triangle types in both the English and Kinyarwanda editions, Year 4 classifies triangles and quadrilaterals, Years 5–6 add cubes, cuboids, polygons and nets, and Senior 1 counts faces, edges and vertices across the solids.

Sudan teaches shapes from Grade 2's straight and curved lines and polygons by their number of sides, through Grade 3's point and segment and the first triangle, rectangle and square, Grade 4's review of point and segment with the ray and the straight line built by extending it, Grade 5's classification of triangles by angles and by sides, Grade 7's polygons, and Grade 8's three-dimensional shapes and prisms.

India carries shapes through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 1–5 and Mathematics Class 6–9 — including shapes around us; describing 3D shapes by touch; copying shapes on a dot grid; faces of a brick (cuboid).


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, IX (Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 9): shapes from the first pictured solids and flat shapes to the classified families — the cube face by face with its net, polygons named by their sides, and the quadrilateral family folded and sorted in Grade 4.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contemos... 1, 2, 3 y 4* (Grade 1), *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2), *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6) and *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7). From its books: from Grade 1: sphere — solids at the circus: the ball is round, has no points, only rolls — such objects are called ESFERAS; from Grade 4: polygons — from tracing solids: a closed polygonal line plus its interior is a polígono; segments named; building on a geoplano (25×25 cm board, 5×5 grid of 4 cm squares, 36 nails); from Grade 7: segments — Segments: part of a line between two endpoints; notation with a bar for the segment and plain letters (or med(·)) for its measure.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 1: rectangle, triangle, square and circle: naming and circling (Lesson 25); from Grade 6: the rhombus: properties, its angles, and drawing one; the parallelogram: properties; the trapezium: bases, legs and the right trapezium; the irregular quadrilateral; the interior angles of a quadrilateral sum to 360 degrees; solids: regular and irregular solids; the cylinder: its two circular bases and curved surface; prisms, named by their bases; right prisms; pyramids, named by their bases; the cone; the right cone, its axis and height; the sphere: centre, radius, diameter, poles and the great circle; from Grade 7: classification of triangles by their sides; median, altitude and angle bisector of a triangle; relation between the sides of a triangle (triangle inequality); polygons; chapter summary and exercises (triangles and polygons); quadrilaterals: parallelogram, rectangle, square, rhombus and trapezium; opposite angles of a parallelogram; diagonals of a parallelogram bisect each other; diagonals of a rectangle are equal; diagonals of a rhombus (perpendicular bisectors); chapter summary and exercises (parallel lines and quadrilaterals); from Grade 8: theorems of the isosceles triangle (base angles and converse); angle bisector theorem; the internal bisectors of a triangle are concurrent; perpendicular bisector of a segment; the perpendicular bisectors of a triangle are concurrent; altitudes of a triangle; medians of a triangle; chapter key points and exercises (triangle theorems).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): 2D and 3D shapes: a regular geometric shape has all sides and angles equal; a square IS a rectangle; the…; Chapter opener, Diskuto: name and classify common 2D shapes (circles, squares, rectangles, triangles) by…; 2D shapes: name and classify 2D shapes by number of sides and corners on geoboards (5 × 5); the number of…; 3D shapes: name and classify 3D shapes by faces, flat or curved; a cylinder rolls in one direction, a sphere…; Unë tani di: build a big form from many shapes with at least one line of symmetry, naming every shape used…; Find and photograph/draw shapes in the classroom and match them to the categories in the pupil book (pupil…. from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Triangles and their types; quadrilaterals (parallelogram, rectangle, rhombus, square, trapezium) and their…; Cube and cuboid; prism and pyramid (3D solids, faces/vertices/edges). from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Opener + 6A: classifying polygons (plans 57–59); : 3D solids and their properties (faces, edges, vertices) (plans 60–61). from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .4 3D solids: nets (hapja) of solids, sketching a box from its net (plan 13); .1 Quadrilaterals: grouping by side/angle properties (plan 56). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Properties of quadrilaterals: square, rhombus, kite (balona), rectangle, parallelogram, trapezium and…. from Grade 11 (teacher's book): Congruence of triangles; congruence cases.

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Triángulos y su clasificación; Clasificación por ángulos; from Grade 8: Introducción a la trigonometría; Clasificación de triángulos; Construcción de un tangram; El cubo; Figuras geométricas (repaso); Características de los cuerpos; from the Compendium's Secundaria 2 section (Grade 8): Introducción a la trigonometría; Las formas en el espacio tridimensional.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Broken line (polyline) and its length; from Grade 6: Ball and sphere: centre, radius, diameter.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part Two: the square, rectangle, triangle and circle named (embroidery-frame table) and recognised in the classroom; mark, colour, match and build the four plane shapes with sticks/plasticine; count each kind in a picture; complete dotted outlines of plane shapes; count resulting rectangles/triangles; the sphere, the cube and the cuboid named from household objects; match objects to solids; count solids in a picture into a table (cubes, spheres, cuboids — most/least); stack pieces in rows and layers and count; unit review: name and colour plane shapes, write each shape's name, divide a drawn square into four equal parts; from Grade 10 Part Two: the triangle — opener; regular polygons — idea; drawing a regular pentagon; regular polygons — exercises.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires identifying, classifying and drawing two-dimensional shapes (square, rectangle, triangle, circle), identifying and classifying three-dimensional shapes (distinguishing cube from cuboid and cylinder from cone), and identifying open and closed curves; Class 2 adds constructing three-dimensional shapes and identifying the two-dimensional shapes forming their faces; Class 3 adds classifying triangles as equilateral, right-angled, isosceles or scalene; Class 4 adds classifying quadrilaterals as square, rectangle or parallelogram. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: triangles defined and typed: right-angled, isosceles, equilateral and obtuse-angled — the obtuse-angled type with the book's further symmetry claim (p. 279) taught corrected, since an isosceles obtuse triangle has two equal sides, two equal angles and one line of symmetry.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 1, Part 1 (student textbook-notebook, 2025); Mathematics 5 (student); Geometry 7; Geometry 8; Geometry 9; Geometry 10: geometric figures: triangle, square, rectangle, circle, oval; comparing figures by shape (same shape / different shape); geometric bodies: cube, pyramid, rectangular box; geometric bodies: sphere, cylinder, cone; the segment (հատված); the triangle as a figure; the polygon (բազմանկյուն); the polygon, continued; picturing the midpoint of a segment; broken line and polygon; undefined and basic concepts of geometry; perpendicular to a line; the isosceles triangle; some properties of the right triangle; midline of a triangle; trapezoid; isosceles and right trapezoids; the concept of a regular polygon; isosceles and equilateral triangles; polygon; parallelogram; rectangle, rhombus; trapezoid.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 41 teacher-evidence items from 16 of the guides (Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4, Book 5, Book 6, Book 10, Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 26, Book 27, Book 28, Book 29, Book 30, Book 31; Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7).

**Book 1:** *Le Monde des Maths 1, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1re année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 1,775,505 bytes, 201 PDF pages. The collection follows the revised curriculum/programmes of July 2020 (32 lessons in 6 units over 4 domains); items are the guide's 32 numbered lessons, printed pp. from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 2:** *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 9,555,550 bytes. Same collection and July-2020 revised programme as Book 1; items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where the sommaire prints them, later lessons cited by sequence (seq.). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 3:** *L'Explicite en Maths CP, Guide pédagogique* (CP = Grade 1) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,092,403 bytes, 116 PDF pages. The explicit-teaching method guide for the pioneer-schools programme; its Chapter 3 gives worked lesson sheets for the SAME 32-lesson Grade 1 programme as Book 1 — a shared programme, two different guides, cited separately, never merged. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 4:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 1, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,559,907 bytes; new edition September 2019, with ministry approval panel. Its contents table lists the same 32-lesson revised programme as Books 1 and 3, in Arabic; the PDF's legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so the contents were read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 5:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE1, Guide pédagogique* (CE1 = Grade 2) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,580,797 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 2 programme corresponding to Book 2. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 6:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1ère année du cycle secondaire collégial* (1AC = Grade 7, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,742,780 bytes, 173 PDF pages. Chapters 1–2 are method framework (excluded in the placements); items are the distinct topics of the 1AC programme distribution (printed p. 73). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 10:** *المرجع في الرياضيات (Al Marjie fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 3, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,309,585 bytes, 351 PDF pages. Its contents (read from rendered pages — legacy font encoding) list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, the Grade 3 programme. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 19:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 5, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 70,552,470 bytes, 330 PDF pages; new edition September 2020, ministry approval serial 515220 (3 Aug 2020). Its contents list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, read from rendered pages (legacy font encoding). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 20:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM2, Guide pédagogique* (CM2 = Grade 5) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,455,488 bytes, 122 PDF pages. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 5 programme, corresponding topic-for-topic to Book 19. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 22:** *Le Monde des Maths 5, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 5e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 5, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,773,818 bytes, 256 PDF pages. Items are its 32 numbered lessons, cited by sequence from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 26:** *Le Monde des Maths 4, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 4e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 4, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 4,953,260 bytes, 190 PDF pages; read in place from its staging copy (never deleted from staging). Items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where printed, later units by sequence. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 27:** *Le Monde des Maths 3, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 3e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 3, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 6,204,899 bytes. Items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where printed, later units by sequence; the Grade 3 programme, corresponding to Book 10. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 28:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE2, Guide pédagogique* (CE2 = Grade 3) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,578,274 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 3 programme, corresponding to Books 10 and 27. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 29:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM1, Guide pédagogique* (CM1 = Grade 4) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,896,704 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 4 programme, corresponding to Books 26 and 30. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 30:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 4, Arabic; contents bilingual Arabic/French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,206,485 bytes, 357 PDF pages; 2019 edition. Its contents list the same 32 lessons as Books 26 and 29, with bilingual titles, read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 31:** *المرجع في الرياضيات (Al Marjie fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذ(ة)* (teacher guide, Grade 2, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 55,959,139 bytes, 393 PDF pages. Items are the distinct rows of its yearly programme table (guide printed pp. 48–50, read from rendered pages), which states the Grade 2 content by domain with learning objectives — a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides, as the placements declare. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 19 items (13 from student books, 6 teacher evidence) from Book S2, Book T2, Book S3, Book T3, Book S5, Book T4, Book S6, Book T5, Book S7, Book T6, Book S10, Book T9.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 20 items (15 from pupil books, 5 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S2, Book S5, Book S6, Book S12, Book S14, Book S17, Book S21, Book T4, Book T7, Book T14, Book T16, Book T33.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 39 items (14 from pupil books, 25 teacher evidence) from Book S2, Book S12, Book S13, Book S14, Book S15, Book S17, Book T8, Book T9, Book T10, Book T11, Book T12, Book T13, Book T14, Book T15, Book T16, Book T17, Book T18, Book T31, Book T32, Book T34, Book T35, Book T36, Book T37, Book T38, Book T39, Book T40.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 15 items from Book 1, Book 3, Book 5, Book 7, Book 9, Book 11, Book 16, Book 17, Book 19, Book 24.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 25 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Programa de matemàtiques cientificotècniques del batxillerat general* (curriculum programme, Grades 11–12) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 242/2025 (BOPA Núm. 83, 9-7-2025); ISBN 978-99920-82-23-2; 4,325,201 bytes, 38 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

Andorra's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 programmes are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand — none exists to hold for the Escola Andorrana — and the Spanish and French systems' maths manuals are list-only names with no files held.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 21 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4, Book 5 and Book 6.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Manual da 1.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 1) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Editora Moderna, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-267-0; 4,293,873 bytes, 81 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1).

**Book 2:** *Matemática — Manual da 2.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 2) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-297-8; 3,437,647 bytes, 56 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 23 calls the minuend “diminuendo ou aditivo” — “aditivo” is wrong for subtraction; the course teaches minuend. Editorial production notes are printed as lesson content on printed p. 50 — production matter, cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 21 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines; the held file is the July 2019 revision, footer “High School Mathematics Curriculum – The Bahamas, 2019”) — Bahamas, Department of Education; 3,766,723 bytes, 219 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.
